# KNOWN_BUGS.md — consolidated bug-sweep ledger

**Purpose.** A single deduplicated register of every distinct finding raised by every past bug
sweep, bug hunt, fraud/security review, UX/usability review, engineering review, and
launch/pilot-readiness audit in this repo. Future sweeps read this first so they don't
rediscover known bugs. Status is verified against the code at the time noted, not trusted from
the source report.

**Last consolidated:** 2026-07-26 (deep-sweep routine — agentic-loop hunt over the deep-sweep lane; see
the "Deep sweep 2026-07-26" section near the bottom and `docs/DEEP-SWEEP-2026-07-26.md`. **Clean run,
zero new findings**: Phase 1 (5 finder lenses) returned 0 candidates, Phase 1.5's PII-across-
representations seam trace found the invariant intact, Phase 3's adversarial API pass found zero new
gaps, and Phase 0.5 re-verified the Notifications/FCM, Edge/abuse, and Auth/identity cluster headers as
all INTACT. Fable-5 was unavailable all run (usage limit) — Phase 1/0.5/3 fell back to the session model
per the routine's explicit fallback policy, noted in the report. One open `claude/*` sibling PR at Phase
0 (#392, UX26-01/02/03) — out of lane, no overlap. Docs-only PR (report + this ledger entry); no code
changed.
Prior: 2026-07-25 (bug-hunt routine — agentic-loop hunt over the bug-hunt lane; see the
"Bug hunt 2026-07-25 night" section near the bottom and `docs/BUG-HUNT-2026-07-25.md` for BH-25a/BH-25b —
both MEDIUM, both fixed same-run with regression tests. BH-25a: `presence:recovered` role:"rider" was
never emitted by `scanPresence()`'s stale-rider recovery loop (the customer→rider mirror of BH-08's
already-working direction), and the customer's socket hook had no listener for it — a heartbeat-lag
self-heal with no fresh GPS push left the customer's "rider may be offline" card stuck until the ride's
next status change; fixed by emitting the event on recovery and wiring a listener that triggers the
existing REST self-heal. BH-25b: `boardSubscribe`'s room re-scope mutated the live, shared `client.rooms`
Set with no per-socket serialization, so two `board:subscribe` calls close together (a real mobile
pattern on background→foreground resume) could interleave and leave a rider joined to both a stale and a
fresh board room; fixed with a per-socket promise-chain serializer, also applied to the confirmed
vulnerable sibling `boardLeave`. `kickRiderFromBoard`'s related-but-distinct TOCTOU (snapshot-based, not
the same live-Set race) was ledgered OPEN as `BH-25-SIB-1` rather than force-fixed, per its own
documented best-effort/non-security design. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Money-fraud**, **Data-integrity**,
and **Ship/infra correctness** cluster headers (rotated to the 3 least-recently-checked) — all INTACT, 0
stale claims. Zero open `claude/*` sibling PRs at Phase 0. `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm test` (1207 API +
mobile, all packages) green.
Prior: 2026-07-21 (wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine — agentic-loop hunt over the WD
lane; see the "Wallet & data-lifecycle audit 2026-07-21" section near the bottom and
`docs/WALLET-DATA-AUDIT-2026-07-21.md` for WD-027/WD-028 — one MEDIUM, one LOW, both fixed same-run with
regression tests. WD-027 (MEDIUM): the Earnings screen's hero total/count came from the server's
unbounded, rider-role-only aggregate while the itemized list beneath was filtered from a capped 50-row
page shared across both the customer and rider roles of the account, so an account disproportionately
active as a customer could see a truthful non-zero total over a silently blank or truncated list with no
explanation; fixed with a new `earningsCoverageNote` helper that renders an honest note whenever the list
under-represents the total. WD-028 (LOW): the admin console's "needs attention" stuck-order row hardcoded a
stale "25+ min" figure DS20-01 (2026-07-20) had deliberately unified away on the backend to 20 min, since
DS20-01's own sibling-sweep never crossed into `apps/admin`; fixed by hoisting the threshold to a new
`STUCK_AFTER_MINUTES` constant in `packages/shared` that both the API and the admin console now derive
from, closing the drift class by construction. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Object-authz/IDOR**, **KYC**,
and **Mobile-journey-dead-ends** cluster headers (rotated to the 3 least-recently-checked, last done by
the deep-sweep/bug-hunt 2026-07-20 runs) — all INTACT, 0 stale claims. Zero open `claude/*` sibling PRs at
Phase 0. `pnpm typecheck` (5 packages, force-executed) + `pnpm test` (1169 API + 45 admin + 120 shared +
524 mobile) + `pnpm build` (`@lynia/api`, `@lynia/admin`) all green.
Prior: 2026-07-21 (deep-sweep routine — agentic-loop hunt over the deep-sweep lane; see
`docs/DEEP-SWEEP-2026-07-21.md` and the "Deep sweep 2026-07-21" section near the bottom for DS21-01/DS21-02 —
one MEDIUM, one LOW, both fixed same-run with regression tests. DS21-01 (MEDIUM): `attachPickupPhoto` /
`attachDeliveryProof` (`order-lifecycle.service.ts`) ran their photo-key read → CAS `updateMany`-on-`status`
→ delete-of-the-pre-write-snapshot's-key with no transaction/row lock, so two concurrent attach calls for the
same order (a client retry duplicating an in-flight request) both read the same pre-write key, both writes
land (the CAS guards only `status`, unchanged), and each request's cleanup compares only its OWN stale
pre-read — so NEITHER purges the OTHER's just-persisted key and the loser's GCS object orphans with zero DB
pointer, unreachable by the right-to-erasure purge (the DS15-03/DS18-01/DS18-03 orphan class, reopened via a
race). Fixed by wrapping both in a `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` row-locked `$transaction` (mirroring
`confirmDelivery`) with a plain post-lock `update` (the lock subsumes the old CAS) and the superseded-object
delete moved post-commit using the key read UNDER THE LOCK — so the second caller blocks, reads the first's
committed key, and purges it. DS21-02 (LOW): the two feed-backing audit actions `order.riders_available_notify`
and `customer.riders_available_notify` (added in UX21-02, read back by `notifications-feed.service.ts` to
render an "A rider's online near you" row) were never added to `admin-audit.service.ts`'s
`RESERVED_AUDIT_ACTIONS`, so an admin-token holder could `POST /admin/audit-actions` to forge a compliance
row / a victim's fake feed notification — the same un-propagated-sibling drift WD-023 fixed once for three KYC
strings. Fixed by reserving both and adding a durable `FEED_READ_ACTIONS ⊆ RESERVED_AUDIT_ACTIONS` invariant
test so the class can't drift a third time. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Auth/identity**, **Notifications/FCM**,
and **Edge/abuse** cluster headers (rotated to the 3 least-recently-checked, last done by the UX 2026-07-20
run) — all INTACT, 0 stale claims. Zero open `claude/*` sibling PRs at Phase 0. `pnpm typecheck` (5 packages) +
`pnpm test` (1169 API + 45 admin + 517 mobile) + `pnpm build` (`@lynia/api`) all green.
Prior: 2026-07-21 (UX-improvements routine — agentic-loop hunt over the UX lane; see
`docs/UX-USABILITY-REVIEW-2026-07-21.md` and the "UX review 2026-07-21" section near the bottom for
UX21-01/UX21-02 — both MEDIUM, both fixed same-run: a zero-`error.tsx` gap that let the admin console's
two busiest bare-form Server Actions (KYC quick-approve, order follow-up note) crash to Next's generic
error screen instead of the console's own inline retryable error text, and a missing durable feed
fallback for the "a rider's online near you" push, the one push type in the app with no KB-FEED-SYNTH

Prior consolidation: 2026-07-20 (bug-hunt routine — agentic-loop hunt over the bug-hunt lane; see
`docs/BUG-HUNT-2026-07-20.md` and the "Bug hunt 2026-07-20 night" section near the bottom for BH-23/24,
two MEDIUM findings, both fixed same-run with regression tests. BH-23: `clearDeviceState()`'s sign-out
wipe (`apps/mobile/src/auth/session.ts`) imported and deleted `RIDER_BID_DRAFT_KEY` but never its sibling
`RIDER_SENT_OFFERS_KEY` (added by BH-21 after this wipe was last touched), so a signed-out rider's
already-sent bids on OTHER open orders survived on a shared device and rehydrated for the next rider
signed in within the same ~90s auction windows — painting a stranger's fare/eta as their own pending offer
and hiding the underlying orders from their board as "already bid". Fixed by adding the missing key to the
delete list, the same gap-shape BH-17 closed for `PICKUP_CHECKLIST_DRAFT_KEY`. BH-24: the mobile `OfferRow`
type declares `rider.ratingAvg` as `string`, but the API sends the underlying Prisma `Float` as a raw JSON
number (unlike `offeredFare`, a `Decimal` the server explicitly `.toString()`s) — confirmed wrong by
contrast with `apps/mobile/src/api/auth.ts`, which correctly types the same field `number` for `/auth/me`.
`order/[id].tsx` persisted the chosen offer's rating verbatim into a `RiderIdentity`, and
`rider-identity.ts`'s restore guard did a strict `typeof === "string"` check that silently reset it to
`"0"` on every reload — showing "★ 0.0" for a real assigned rider on the live-tracking card. Fixed by
coercing to `String(...)` at the write site and making the restore guard also accept (and self-heal) a
raw JSON number. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **KYC**, **Data-integrity**, and **Mobile-journey-dead-ends**
cluster headers (rotated to the three least-recently checked — all three last verified by yesterday's
2026-07-19 bug-hunt/UX runs, while the other six were re-checked earlier today by the deep-sweep and UX
routines) — all 3 INTACT, 0 stale claims. Zero open `claude/*` sibling PRs at Phase 0.
`pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm test` (1161 API + 517 mobile) green.
Prior: 2026-07-20 (deep-sweep routine — agentic-loop hunt over the deep-sweep lane; see
`docs/DEEP-SWEEP-2026-07-20.md` and the "Deep sweep 2026-07-20" section near the bottom for DS20-01…03 —
one MEDIUM, one HIGH, one LOW, all fixed same-run with regression tests. DS20-01 (MEDIUM): two separate
`STUCK_AFTER_MS` constants (`admin.service.ts` 25 min off `order.updatedAt` vs `admin.shared.ts` 20 min off
the last `OrderEvent.createdAt`) had drifted, so the same order could read "stuck" on its detail page yet not
appear in the ops-dashboard alert (a non-status write like `attachPickupPhoto` bumps `updatedAt` without an
`OrderEvent`); both call sites now import the single 20-min `admin.shared.STUCK_AFTER_MS`, with the two
intentional data-source clocks preserved + commented. DS20-02 (HIGH): the WS `rider:location` handler
`@MessageBody`-typed but never runtime-validated the untrusted socket JSON, broadcasting the raw lat/lng to
the customer's map (`coalescePositionEmit`) and persisting it (`recordFix`) with none of the bounded-lat/lng
zod validation every sibling REST path enforces — now `safeParse`d through a new shared `RiderLocationEvent`
schema (rejects out-of-range/NaN before emit/persist) with the `recordFix` call try/catch-wrapped like the
REST heartbeat caller. DS20-03 (LOW hardening): `riders.cooldownUntil`'s two independent writers (3rd
cancel-strike + 3rd dispute-strike) each hardcoded their own equal 2h literal with an unconditional
overwrite, so a future drift would let the shorter one silently truncate a longer sibling cooldown; hoisted a
single `RIDER_STRIKE_COOLDOWN_MS` into `policy.ts` and made both writes take the later of the fresh window
and any existing future cooldown (read under the already-held row lock), so a cooldown can never shorten.
Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Object-authz/IDOR**, **Ship/infra correctness**, and **Money-fraud** cluster
headers (rotated away from Auth/identity / Notifications-FCM / Edge/abuse and KYC / Mobile-journey-dead-ends
just checked by the two 2026-07-20/-19 UX runs) — all 3 INTACT/MOOT-confirmed, 0 stale claims. Zero open
`claude/*` sibling PRs at Phase 0. `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm test` (1114 API + 21 admin + 492 mobile) +
`pnpm build` all green.
Prior: 2026-07-20 (UX-improvements routine — agentic-loop hunt over the UX lane; see
`docs/UX-USABILITY-REVIEW-2026-07-20.md` and the "UX review 2026-07-20" section near the bottom for
UX20-01…04, four MEDIUM findings, all fixed same-run with regression tests where testable. UX20-01 is a
query-error-as-negative-state gap: the customer home screen's `activeOrderQ` and the rider dashboard's
`activeQ` never read `.isError`, so a real query error (non-2xx or 2 exhausted retries) collapsed to the
exact same `null` as a genuine "no active order/job" — a rider with an assigned job or a customer with a
live order who hit this on a flaky connection saw the ordinary browse/board UI with zero indication the
check failed and zero way back; both screens now show a compact error-with-retry banner instead. UX20-02
and UX20-03 are the same defect class (hardcoded "0%/launch period" commission copy that never reads the
live, operator-flippable `COMMISSION_RATE_PCT`) found independently by two finder lenses: the admin
overview's needs-attention "Commission is 0%" row was structurally incapable of ever changing (the
`Overview` API response had no rate field at all — `AdminService.overview()` now resolves and returns the
live rate); the rider Earnings screen's explainer card and three strings on the admin Commission page kept
insisting "0%... still 0%" beside sibling copy on the SAME screens that already correctly read the live
rate — all 4 sites now gate on it. UX20-04 is a notification-coherence gap: `adjustFare`'s post-commit push
had zero durable feed fallback (its `order.fare_adjust` audit is order-targeted, so it matched neither the
per-event `FEED_NOTICES` synthesis, which needs an `OrderEvent` that `adjustFare` never writes, nor the
profile-targeted `ACCOUNT_FEED_ACTIONS` synthesis) — a missed push left zero durable record for either
party that their fare was corrected; a new synthesis block recovers it for both parties, mirroring the
existing `order.rider_standing_notice` pattern. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Auth/identity**,
**Notifications/FCM**, and **Edge/abuse** cluster headers (rotated away from KYC/Object-authz-IDOR/
Mobile-journey-dead-ends, which the 2026-07-19 UX run picked) — all three INTACT, 0/6 stale claims. Two
open sibling PRs at Phase 0 (#339 bug-hunt BH-21/22, #338 performance wave-2) — neither in the UX lane;
both branches' `KNOWN_BUGS.md` diffs read against `main` and contain only their own lane's entries, no
overlap.
Prior: 2026-07-19 (wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine — agentic-loop hunt over the WD
lane; see the "Wallet & data-lifecycle audit 2026-07-19" section near the bottom and
`docs/WALLET-DATA-AUDIT-2026-07-19.md` for WD-024…WD-026 — one HIGH and two MEDIUM findings, all fixed
same-run with regression tests. WD-024 and WD-026 share one root cause: the admin overview's "Completed
today"/"Fares today"/completion-rate KPIs (`AdminService.overview()`) gated on the `deliveredAt`/
`undeliveredAt` timestamps alone rather than the order's CURRENT status, so a post-delivery admin cancel
(WD-024, `delivered` is deliberately non-terminal) kept a cancelled order counting as completed forever,
and `adjudicateDelivered`'s force-complete of a disputed `undelivered` order (WD-026) — which stamps
`completedAt` but never `deliveredAt`, and never clears `undeliveredAt` — was permanently invisible to
`today.completed`/`today.fares` while staying permanently double-counted as a failure. Both fixed by adding
the matching `status` filter alongside each timestamp gate, mirroring the convention `settlements.service.ts`
already used. WD-025 is the same client-cache-omission class WD-022 (07-17) fixed, on a query key that
run's sibling-sweep didn't check: the Earnings tab's commission-balance row (`CommissionRow` →
`useWallet()`) was never invalidated by the shared `invalidateRiderJobQueries` funnel a ride completion
funnels through, so a rider glancing at Earnings shortly before completing a delivery could see the
pre-debit balance for up to 30s with no staleness indicator — fixed by adding the two wallet keys to that
one shared funnel. Phase-0.5 re-verified the **Money-fraud** (MOOT), **Ship/infra correctness**, and
**Auth/identity** cluster headers (the three least-recently rotated — the prior three routines had already
covered the other six today/yesterday) — all three INTACT, 0 stale claims. Zero open sibling PRs at
Phase 0.
Prior: 2026-07-19 (deep-sweep routine — orthogonal backend-correctness/concurrency hunt +
deep-sweep-owned cross-lane seams pass + adversarial API pass; see the "Deep sweep 2026-07-19" section near
the bottom and `docs/DEEP-SWEEP-2026-07-19.md` for DS19-01…02 — one MEDIUM and one LOW, both fixed same-run

(`accountStatus`/`onHold`/`isOnline`/`kycStatus`) across all 14 standing writers: DS19-01 (MEDIUM) is two
sibling `applyReliabilityDelta` call sites — `rate()`'s customer-rating flow and `cancel()`'s
below-strike-limit branch — that trip an `onHold` reliability hold without forcing the rider offline or
evicting them from the live-supply planes (the standing demotion `markUndelivered` and the two DS17-02
strike-limit paths already handle correctly), leaving a held-but-`isOnline:true` rider a GEOSEARCH/board
ghost; DS19-02 (LOW) is `eraseAccount`'s post-commit eviction being geo-only (`evictRiderFromGeo`) instead
of the both-planes `evictRiderFromSupply` funnel every other demotion path uses, so an already-open
WebSocket kept its board rooms past erasure. The same seam trace flagged **`KB-HOLD-SESSION-SCOPE`**
(now OPEN, LOW) — session/token revocation on demotion is implemented only for admin suspend/ban, and
`refresh()` re-checks only `accountStatus`, so a velocity-held/KYC-lapsed rider keeps authenticated API
access indefinitely; logged for a product decision, deliberately NOT fixed speculatively. Phase-0.5
re-verified the **Notifications/FCM** and **Edge/abuse** cluster headers (rotated away from the KYC/
Object-authz/Mobile-journey-dead-ends and Auth-identity/Data-integrity/Money-fraud clusters the prior two
routines re-checked) — both INTACT, 0 stale claims. Phase 1 (`lane-bug-hunt`, 5 lenses) returned 0
candidates; Phase 3 adversarial API pass came back clean. Zero open sibling PRs at Phase 0.

| KB-HOLD-SESSION-SCOPE | **Session/token revocation on rider demotion is scoped only to admin suspend/ban, not to `onHold`/KYC-lapse** (surfaced by the 2026-07-19 deep-sweep rider-standing seam trace). `suspendRider`/`banRider` revoke sessions in-transaction (`session.updateMany({profileId, revokedAt:null}, {revokedAt})`, IR16-01) and `auth.service.ts refresh()` re-reads `s.profile.rider.accountStatus` as a backstop (hard-rejects suspended/banned) — but `refresh()` re-checks **only `accountStatus`**, never `onHold`/`kycStatus`. So a velocity-held / reliability-held / KYC-lapsed (expired/failed) rider keeps full authenticated API access, including token refresh, **indefinitely** — the supply-plane eviction + `isOnline:false` (DS17-02 / DS19-01 / DS19-02) remove them from matching/board, but not from the authenticated API surface. This MAY be intentional: a held/lapsed rider plausibly needs to stay logged in to retry KYC, contact support, or finish an already-active delivery. **Not a proven defect — a flagged asymmetry needing a product decision** on whether hold/KYC-lapse should also cut sessions BEFORE any code change (a session-revoke here without that call is exactly the speculative behavior change this flag warns against). | `apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` (`refresh()`, ~line 360/377 — `accountStatus`-only recheck), `apps/api/src/admin/admin-riders.service.ts` (`suspendRider`/`banRider` in-tx `session.updateMany` revoke sites, ~line 227/335) | LOW | 1 | Found by the 2026-07-19 deep-sweep Phase-1.5 rider-standing seam pass. **Needs a product decision** on whether a hold/KYC-lapse demotion should revoke sessions (like ban/suspend) or deliberately preserve them (retry-KYC / support / active-delivery access). No code change made — logged OPEN pending that call. |

But if a `boardSubscribe` call joins a fresh geo room AFTER the kick's snapshot was taken, that just-joined room survives the kick — a suspended/held rider keeps receiving board pushes on that one room until the next reconnect. | `apps/api/src/tracking/tracking.gateway.ts` (`kickRiderFromBoard`) | LOW | 1 | Found by the 2026-07-25 bug-hunt routine's BH-25b sibling-sweep. Left OPEN rather than adding cross-instance locking across the `fetchSockets()` remote-socket boundary — the method's own docstring already documents this path as best-effort/non-security ("a missed kick just means a few stale board cards until the next reconnect, never a bypass"; every real bid path re-gates standing independently), so a distributed lock would be disproportionate to a cosmetic, self-healing gap. Owning lane: BH (bug hunting) — revisit if `kickRiderFromBoard` ever gains a security-relevant purpose. |
| LC-B-SIB-1 | **`adjudicateDelivered` charges Express ride-commission on a merchant/food order's full goods+delivery total, missing the `orderType==="parcel"` guard its siblings have.** `OrderLifecycleService.rate()` and `completeOrder()` both gate `chargeCommission` behind `if (order.orderType === "parcel")` with an explicit comment that a merchant order's own commission is C4's ledger, not the Express wallet, and that `chargeCommission` assumes a parcel fare basis. `AdminOrdersService.adjudicateDelivered` never selects `orderType` and has no such guard, so force-completing an `undelivered` merchant/food order (reachable today via `FoodDebtService.reportNoShow`/`reportCustomerRefused` → the shared `markUndelivered`, then the admin "Mark delivered (code bypass)" button, which also has no `orderType` gate) would charge commission off the full goods+delivery total instead of a delivery-only basis — dormant at today's 0% rate (only corrupts the `commission_shadow` calibration signal), but a real ledger mispricing the moment the rate goes non-zero. **Not fixed here** — found incidentally by an LC loop B tooling misconfiguration that ran the wallet-lane finder set instead of the intended Lane B re-render lenses (adversarially verified 3/3 REAL-high all the same); this touches a money/commission path outside Lane B's runtime-perf mandate and needs the sensitive-lane 4-question treatment. | `apps/api/src/admin/admin-orders.service.ts:217-269` (`adjudicateDelivered`); siblings with the guard: `apps/api/src/orders/order-lifecycle.service.ts` `rate()`/`completeOrder()`; also-unguarded: `apps/admin/app/orders/[id]/page.tsx:461-465` ("Mark delivered (code bypass)" button) | HIGH (dormant at 0% commission) | — | **OPEN — flagged for Lane D or the wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine** (whichever fires next) to triage, assign a proper `WD-`/`LC-D` id, and fix with the sensitive-money-path treatment (4-question doctrine, `docs/ROUTINES.md`). |
| LC-B-SIB-2 | **Rider Money-tab wallet ledger silently truncates at 25 entries with no pagination affordance.** `useWalletLedger()` (`apps/mobile/src/query/use-wallet.ts:42`) always calls `getWalletLedger()` with no cursor; `WalletService.getLedger` caps every response at `LEDGER_PAGE_SIZE=25` and returns a `nextCursor`, but the cursor is never read anywhere in the mobile app (the rider Money tab, `apps/mobile/app/rider/(tabs)/money.tsx`, renders `page.entries` directly with no "load more"/`onEndReached`). A rider with >25 lifetime wallet events (one commission debit per job plus every top-up — plausible for an active rider) permanently loses visibility into older deductions, with zero UI signal anything is missing, contradicting the screen's own copy ("every deduction shows up here"). **The admin-side sibling of this same truncation shape is already known and OPEN as `LC-D07`** (`apps/admin/app/riders/[id]/page.tsx:269`, hardcoded `take:20`) — this is the mobile-client instance, not previously ledgered. Found incidentally by the same LC loop B tooling misconfiguration as `LC-B-SIB-1` (adversarially verified 3/3 REAL-high). | `apps/mobile/src/query/use-wallet.ts:42`, `apps/mobile/app/rider/(tabs)/money.tsx` | MEDIUM | — | **OPEN — flagged for Lane D** (extends `LC-D07`'s scope to the mobile client) or the wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine. Not fixed here — out of Lane B's runtime-perf mandate. |
| LC-B-SIB-3 | **Commission ledger receipt's "checkable math" text doesn't match the amount actually debited when the WD-012 anti-lowball fare floor applies.** `chargeCommission` bills on `commissionBasis(agreedFare, suggestedFare) = max(agreedFare, 0.5×suggestedFare)` (`wallet.service.ts:235`) but writes the RAW `agreedFare` (not the basis) into the `CommissionLedger.fare` column (`wallet.service.ts:259-263`). `toEntry()` then builds the rider-facing receipt string from that raw fare — `meta = "${rate}% of $${fare.toFixed(2)}"` (`wallet.service.ts:151`) — while `amount` is the real debit computed off the basis. The rider Money tab (`apps/mobile/app/rider/(tabs)/money.tsx:299`) renders that `meta` text next to `entry.amount` on the same `LedgerRow`, whose own doc comment claims it shows "the checkable math the server stored" so the rider "can always check the math." Whenever the floor bites (`suggestedFare > 2× agreedFare`), the label's implied amount and the real debit diverge with no explanation — e.g. `agreedFare=$1.00`, `suggestedFare=$10.00`, 10% rate: basis=`max(1, 5)=5`, real debit `-$0.50` (confirmed by the existing test at `wallet.service.spec.ts:203-218`), but the receipt text reads "10% of $1.00" (=$0.10 by the rider's own arithmetic) sitting beside "−$0.50" — a rider checking the math concludes they were overcharged 5×. Notably `useWallet()` one hook away in the same `money.tsx` component *does* check `isError` correctly (`:187`), showing the fix pattern was known but not applied consistently. Found incidentally by an LC loop D tooling misconfiguration (the `lane-bug-hunt` workflow's custom-lane `args` object was not honored by the underlying script invocation and silently ran the hardcoded wallet lane instead of the intended D-T2 merchant-journey lenses — same class of misconfiguration as `LC-B-SIB-1..4`; adversarially verified 3/3 REAL-high on the primary finding). | `apps/mobile/app/rider/(tabs)/money.tsx:143,269-282`; siblings: `apps/mobile/app/food/search.tsx:18-27,60-78`, `apps/mobile/app/rider/food-offer.tsx:53-66`, `apps/mobile/app/food/checkout.tsx:34,88-102`, `apps/mobile/app/rider/food-job.tsx:71-77,474-496` | MEDIUM | — | **OPEN — flagged for the bug-hunt or UX-improvements routine** (mobile-client error/empty-state class bug, not a money-mutation path — no sensitive-lane treatment needed for the fix itself, just isError wiring + a retry affordance at each site). Not fixed here — out of Lane D's D-T2 merchant-journey territory this run. |
| LC-D-SIB-2 | **Rider suspend/ban and customer hold/lift admin actions CAS-guard against a concurrent OTHER change but not against re-applying the SAME action — a lost-response retry double-writes the audit trail and re-fires customer/rider pushes.** `suspendRider`/`banRider` (`admin-riders.service.ts:209,332`) and `AdminCustomersService.holdCustomer`/`liftCustomerHold` (`admin-customers.service.ts:193,229`) all read the current status then CAS `updateMany({where:{...,status: currentStatus}})` — a WHERE that trivially matches when the just-read status already equals the target, so replaying an already-applied action (e.g. an admin's lost-response retry after suspending an already-SUSPENDED rider) writes a second `AuditLog` row with the retry's own possibly-different reason text, and re-fires the rider "Account paused"/"Account blocked" push plus every active-order customer's "we're reviewing this trip" push a second time. The sibling "undo" actions in the same files (`liftRider`, `clearHold`, `AdminCustomersService.liftCashBan`) already have the correct explicit precondition guard (e.g. `if (!rider.onHold) throw`) — proving the fix pattern is known in this codebase, just not applied to the four "apply" actions and one "lift" outlier. Not fixed here — out of Lane D's D-T2 merchant-journey territory this run, and a money/trust-lane admin action shouldn't ship unreviewed from an off-mandate tooling misfire. |
| LC-D-SIB-3 | **`disputeCash`'s check-then-freeze isn't guarded against a concurrent `confirmRiderCash` — a resolved cash handshake can be falsely frozen, and a sibling TOCTOU exists in `settleDebt`.** `disputeCash()` (`food-debt.service.ts:152-163`) reads `{customerCashConfirmedAt, riderCashConfirmedAt}` outside any transaction, then calls the private `freeze()` helper, whose own CAS (`:191-207`) only re-checks `cashHandshakeFrozenAt: null` — never re-checking `riderCashConfirmedAt`. If the same rider's `confirmRiderCash()` commits in the window between `disputeCash`'s read and its `freeze()` call, `freeze()` still succeeds, leaving the order with `cashHandshakeFrozenAt` SET even though `riderCashConfirmedAt` is also SET — both parties get a false "we've flagged this order" push despite a clean confirm, and the order is permanently stuck (admin's `resolveHandshake` refuses to touch an already-resolved handshake, and the dispute queue's own filter requires `riderCashConfirmedAt:null`, so it never resurfaces for ops either). Sibling TOCTOU, same root shape: `settleDebt` (`:353-378`, called from `confirmReturnedCash`/`confirmGoodsReturned`/`reportNonReturn`) CASes only on `debtStatus:"open"`, never re-checking `order.status` — a concurrent `AdminOrdersService.adjudicateUndelivered` (CAS on `status`) can commit in the gap, leaving an order `debtStatus:"settled_goods"` (goods "returned unsold") while `status:"completed"` (adjudicated as successfully delivered), a self-contradictory state. Found incidentally by the same LC loop D tooling misconfiguration as `LC-D-SIB-1` (adversarially verified 3/3 REAL-high). | `apps/api/src/merchant/food-debt.service.ts:191-207` (`freeze`, called from `disputeCash:152-163` and `sweepFrozenHandshakes:168-189`); `:353-378` (`settleDebt`, new sibling) | MEDIUM | — | **OPEN — flagged for the wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine** (cash-handshake reconciliation, sensitive-lane doctrine — needs the 4-question treatment: the fix re-checks the second field inside the same CAS). Not fixed here — out of Lane D's D-T2 merchant-journey territory this run. |
| LC-D-SIB-4 | **`logDoorstepCall`'s read-modify-write on `noShowCallTimestamps` has no CAS — concurrent calls (a flaky-network double-tap) lose a logged call.** `logDoorstepCall()` (`food-debt.service.ts:213-225`) reads `order.noShowCallTimestamps`, appends one `Date` in JS, then writes the whole array back with a plain `prisma.order.update` — no CAS on the prior array length, no transaction, no row lock. Two near-simultaneous calls both read the same N-length array and each write back N+1; the second write clobbers the first, so only one new timestamp survives. `reportNoShow()` gates on `calls.length < noShowMinCalls` (2) and an 8-minute window from the earliest call — losing a call under this race can leave a rider who genuinely made 2+ doorstep calls short of the recorded minimum, blocking a legitimate no-show report. Found incidentally by the same LC loop D tooling misconfiguration as `LC-D-SIB-1` (adversarially verified 3/3 REAL-high). | `apps/api/src/merchant/food-debt.service.ts:213-225` | LOW | — | **OPEN — flagged for the wallet & data-lifecycle audit routine** (no-show/debt determination feeds rider standing, sensitive-adjacent). Not fixed here — out of Lane D's D-T2 merchant-journey territory this run; fix is a straightforward CAS on array length inside a transaction. |

### Day-0 LC sweep (2026-08-01) — Harare low-connectivity program

Bootstrap audit for `docs/plans/2026-08-01-low-connectivity-program.md`. Full report +
verification-status honesty note: `docs/LC-DAY0-AUDIT-2026-08-01.md`. Confirmed defects are OPEN
and stand **first** on their owning LC lane's checklist (fixed next firing with a regression test;
sensitive paths get the 4-question treatment). Infra items are READ-ONLY for the LC lanes (founder
applies) — all four `LC-INF*` items were applied by the founder in PR #470 (2026-08-01, reviewed
interactively) the same day they were found. Three trivial fixes shipped in the bootstrap PR
(LC-A01 font barrel −5.4 MB, LC-A02 dead dep, LC-D01 AD_ID plugin regression).

| ID | Description | Area | Sev | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LC-C01 | Memorystore outage HANGS the live API (no `commandTimeout`, default `enableOfflineQueue:true` → awaited Redis commands queue forever; every "best-effort fallback" catch unreachable; hung requests saturate Cloud Run slots). Latent since DS15-01. | `apps/api/src/common/redis.ts:26` | **CRITICAL** | **FIXED** (opt-in `REDIS_FAIL_FAST` = `enableOfflineQueue:false` + 2s `commandTimeout`, applied to the OTP/rate-limit, MicroCache-L2, and tracking geo/position request-path clients; the Socket.IO pub/sub adapter keeps the default offline-queue. Regression spec in `redis.spec.ts`. docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-01.md) |
| LC-C02 | No request timeout in the merchant API client — one stalled 2G request freezes the kitchen board, header still "Connected". | `apps/merchant/app/lib/api-client.ts:76` | HIGH | **FIXED** (`MERCHANT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000` via `AbortSignal.timeout` on every fetch call site — `rawFetch`, `doRefresh`, and both `authedFetch` attempts. Regression tests in `api-client.test.ts`. docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-02.md) |
| LC-C03 | Transient blip/5xx on `/auth/refresh` signs the merchant out mid-shift. | `apps/merchant/app/lib/api-client.ts:166` | HIGH | **FIXED** (`doRefresh` now returns a `RefreshOutcome` — `refreshed` / `dead` (401/403) / `transient` (network error, timeout, 5xx) — instead of collapsing all three to `null`; `authedFetch` clears the session only on `dead`. Regression tests in `api-client.test.ts`. docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-02.md) |
| LC-C04 | Hung request permanently freezes the kitchen board — in-flight latch cleared only in `finally`, no fetch timeout; alarm never rings for the 3-min accept window. | `apps/merchant/app/lib/use-queue-poll.ts:31` | CRITICAL | **FIXED** (paired with LC-C02's timeout: a stalled request now always resolves within 10s so `finally` clears the latch; added a self-healing `InflightLatch` backstop that force-clears after 25s regardless, plus an independent periodic `/healthz` probe in `reachability.ts` (`ACTIVE_PROBE_INTERVAL_MS`) that detects an outage even with zero in-flight app traffic. Regression tests in `inflight-latch.test.ts` + `reachability.test.ts`. docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-02.md) |
| LC-C05 | Post-mutation refetch dropped when a poll is in flight + out-of-order responses with no sequencing guard → answered NEW ORDER takeover resurrects and re-rings. | `apps/merchant/app/lib/use-queue-poll.ts:33` | MEDIUM | **FIXED** (2026-08-02) — a refetch requested while one is already in flight (the common case: `QueueBoard`'s post-accept/reject `refetch()` landing mid-interval-poll) now sets a pending flag and is coalesced into one more round the instant the in-flight request settles, instead of being silently dropped by the latch. A monotonic per-hook generation counter tags every request; only the response matching the latest generation is applied to state, so a stale, out-of-order response (reachable via the `InflightLatch`'s own 25s stale-override backstop) can no longer clobber a fresher one. Regression tests in `use-queue-poll.test.ts` (confirmed both fail against the pre-fix code). docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-02b.md |
| LC-B04 | Unbounded React render loop for the whole shift (unmemoized context value + tick-bumping alarm effect) — one A53 core pegged on a Go-class tablet. | `apps/merchant/app/components/KitchenConnectionProvider.tsx:112` | CRITICAL | **FIXED** (`value`/`alarm` memoized with `useMemo`; `ring()`/`silence()`/`testRing()`/`arm()` now bump the `alarmTick` re-render trigger only on an actual controller-state transition instead of unconditionally, which is what let the queue screen's `[unansweredCount, alarm]` alarm-sync effect re-fire itself forever. Render-count regression pin in `KitchenConnectionProvider.test.tsx` — confirmed it hangs against the pre-fix code. docs/LC-B-REPORT-2026-08-02.md) |
| LC-B05 | Customer home tab's (and sibling `send.tsx`'s) active-order cache write-back into `orderKey(id)` was unguarded by the `homeFocused` state that already gates its own poll, so an unrelated foreground-refetch of `activeOrderQ` while blurred beneath `/order/[id]` could blindly overwrite the SAME cache entry `use-order-socket.ts` streams live GPS position/status into — bypassing that hook's own anti-rollback guard and rolling the rider's pin backward on the live tracking map for one tick. Found by the B-T2 re-render audit. | `apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/home.tsx:138-146`, `apps/mobile/app/send.tsx:157-160` | MEDIUM | **FIXED** (2026-08-02) — both write-back effects gated on `homeFocused`, mirroring the existing poll gate: a write only ever seeds the cache ahead of navigating TO the order screen, never clobbers it while already there. Found by the B-T2 re-render audit. | `apps/mobile/app/food/search.tsx:19`, `apps/mobile/app/food/index.tsx:17`, `apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/home.tsx` (`RestaurantsRail`) | MEDIUM | **FIXED** (2026-08-02) — new shared `useNow(intervalMs=60_000)` hook (`apps/mobile/src/logic/use-now.ts`), a real interval-driven clock, wired into all three sites. The two swallowing mutations — `HoursPage.onToggleBusy` and `MenuPage.onClearOos` ("back in stock"), both a bare `try/finally` with no `catch` — now surface the failure as a retryable inline error, mirroring the sibling error-handling pattern already used elsewhere on both pages. | LC-D12 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (recovery/resilience lens): every one of Queue/Menu/Shop/Hours/Statement's initial data loads had no retry affordance and nothing re-triggered them on reconnect — the only escape from a failed load was navigating to a different tab and back, remounting the page. Worst on Queue: `useQueuePoll(state.status === "ready")` gates the whole order poll + alarm loop behind that one-time load, so a single dropped `/merchant/me` call at mount (a tablet reboot reconnecting in a dead zone, or the merchant walking to a bad-signal corner of the kitchen) permanently killed the alarm with zero recovery, even once the network fully recovered — the same failure class LC-C04/LC-D02 were rated CRITICAL for, one level up the mount chain. | `apps/merchant/app/(app)/queue/page.tsx`, `menu/page.tsx`, `shop/page.tsx`, `hours/page.tsx`, `statement/page.tsx` | HIGH (CRITICAL on Queue specifically) | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — all five pages get a manual Retry button on their error state; Queue additionally auto-retries the instant `ReachabilityStore` reports reachable again while still stuck on the error screen, since it's the alarm loop itself and can't wait on the merchant noticing. Regression tests in `queue/page.test.tsx` (new), `menu/page.test.tsx`, the new `shop/page.test.tsx` and `statement/page.test.tsx`. See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D13 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (shift/reachability lens): `ReachabilityStore.scheduleActiveProbe()`'s success branch called a bare `scheduleActiveProbe()` instead of going through the full `reportReachable()` path. A concurrent `reportUnreachable()` (a failed app request) racing an already-in-flight active probe could flip `state.reachable` to false while `probing` was still true, causing `scheduleProbe()` (called by that `reportUnreachable()`) to bail out on the `probing` guard; when the in-flight probe then resolved `ok`, the bare `scheduleActiveProbe()` call's own `!state.reachable` guard rejected it too — leaving no timer of any kind armed, permanently. The header kept showing "CONNECTION LOST… reconnecting automatically (attempt N)" with `attempt` frozen forever, actively lying about an automatic reconnect that had stopped happening, and `actionsDisabled` froze every mutating control app-wide with no self-heal short of a page reload. | `apps/merchant/app/lib/reachability.ts` (`scheduleActiveProbe`) | HIGH | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — the success branch now calls `this.reportReachable()`, which always reschedules from whatever the current state actually is instead of bailing on a stale guard check. Regression test in `reachability.test.ts` (constructs the exact race and asserts the store still recovers). See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D14 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (shift/reachability lens): `doRefresh`'s "transient" `/auth/refresh` outcome collapsed a genuine network-level throw (the server was NOT reached) and a live 5xx response (the server WAS reached) into one value with no way for `authedFetch` to tell them apart — so a network-level throw inside `doRefresh`'s own fetch call was never reported into `ReachabilityStore` at all (the call site's own comment claimed "this function's own request attempts... distinguish a genuine network-level failure," which is false for a throw inside a THIRD request `doRefresh` makes). The header could keep showing "Connected" for up to the 20s active-probe interval while the link was actually down, on any page with no poller (Hours/Shop/Menu/Statement). | `apps/merchant/app/lib/api-client.ts` (`doRefresh`, `authedFetch`) | MEDIUM | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — `RefreshOutcome`'s `transient` variant now carries a `networkError: boolean` flag set at both `doRefresh` return sites, and `authedFetch` reports `reportUnreachable()`/`reportReachable()` accordingly instead of doing nothing. Regression tests in `api-client.test.ts`. See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D15 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (recovery/resilience lens): `uploadPhotoBlob` (the shop banner/logo and dish photo PUT) deliberately bypasses `authedFetch` (a raw binary upload with no bearer token, per its own comment) — but that meant it was also a blind spot for the shared `ReachabilityStore`: a network-level drop mid-upload (PhotoPicker's own error+retry UI handled the upload itself fine) never flipped the connectivity pill, unlike every other authenticated call in the app. | `apps/merchant/app/lib/menu-api.ts` (`uploadPhotoBlob`) | LOW | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — now calls `reportUnreachable()`/`reportReachable()` directly, mirroring `authedFetch`'s own LC-D04 wiring. Regression tests in the new `menu-api.test.ts`. See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D16 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (order-intake lens): `RiderSecuredTakeover` rendered with no `key` in `QueueBoard.tsx`, unlike its sibling takeovers (`NewOrderTakeover key={active.id}`, `NoRiderHoldTakeover key={holdToShow.id}`). If a second order was already rider-secured at the moment the first was dismissed (no intervening render where `securedToShow` was null — plausible any time dispatch secures two riders back-to-back during a busy multi-order stretch), React reused the same component instance instead of remounting it: the local `pickupCode` state kept the FIRST order's real code while the label switched to the SECOND order, and stayed wrong indefinitely if the second order's own `revealPickupCode()` call errored. A merchant handing off the second order could read out the first order's pickup code at the counter — a security-relevant secret handed to the wrong rider. | `apps/merchant/app/components/queue/QueueBoard.tsx` | HIGH | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — added `key={securedToShow.id}`, forcing a full remount (and state reset) at the order boundary exactly like its siblings. Regression test in `QueueBoard.test.tsx` (constructs the two-simultaneously-secured race with a deferred second reveal and asserts the stale code never shows). See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D17 | D-T2 merchant journey sweep (order-intake lens): `withRefetch`'s `refetch()` call was fire-and-forget — `NewOrderTakeover.submitAccept`/`submitReject`'s `await onAccept(...)` resolved as soon as the mutation's own HTTP round trip finished, well before the follow-up queue GET (the thing that actually removes the order from `awaitingAccept`) landed. The Accept/Reject buttons re-enabled on a screen that still visually showed the same, just-handled order for however long the refetch round trip took (1-2+ seconds plausible on this program's 300-600ms-RTT/dead-zone links), inviting a "nothing happened, tap again" retry that the server's atomic per-order CAS turned into a scary but harmless 409 on an order the merchant had in fact just handled correctly — plus a wasted request on a metered link. Self-healing (not a permanent limbo), but a real, reproducible confusion defect. | `apps/merchant/app/components/queue/QueueBoard.tsx` (`withRefetch`), `apps/merchant/app/lib/use-queue-poll.ts` (`fetchOnce`/`refetch`) | MEDIUM | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — `refetch()` is now awaited end-to-end: `useQueuePoll`'s `fetchOnce` returns its own promise (was voided), and a caller that can't acquire the in-flight latch (the coalesced case) now queues a resolver fired only once its coalesced follow-up fetch actually completes, instead of resolving immediately. Extends the same protection to every other `withRefetch`-wrapped action (mark-ready, payment actions, hold resume/cancel, returns), not just accept/reject. Regression tests in `QueueBoard.test.tsx` and `use-queue-poll.test.ts`. See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03c.md. |
| LC-D18 | D-T3 notification/deep-link coherence audit: a cold-start push tap (app fully killed, launched by the tap) was routed by usePushRegistration's own `getLastNotificationResponseAsync()` read, entirely independent of `app/index.tsx`'s own cold-boot `<Redirect>` — both fired a navigation on first mount with no coordination between them, so whichever resolved second silently won. Since `index.tsx`'s own async reads (session load, onboarding flag, role pref — all local SecureStore/AsyncStorage) have no ordering guarantee against the notification read, a boot redirect that resolved after the push's navigation could silently clobber it — landing a rider who tapped "You got the job" (or a customer who tapped "Delivered — tap to rate") back on the default board/home screen instead, with no error and no visible reason. Latent on every cold start regardless of network condition (both sides are local reads), but exactly the "arrives late, strands the user" shape this territory was scoped to find: the OS tray can hold a notification for hours on this program's dead-zone-prone devices, so the tap and the eventual boot resolution are never causally ordered. | `apps/mobile/src/push/use-push-registration.ts` (former cold-start effect), `apps/mobile/app/index.tsx` | HIGH | **FIXED** (LC loop D, 2026-08-03) — the cold-start notification read moved entirely into `index.tsx`'s own boot sequence (via a new memoized `consumeColdStartResponse()` in `push.ts`, safe to await from a single caller), folded into ONE decision (`bootRedirectTarget`, `src/logic/boot-route.ts`) alongside the ordinary boot destination — a deep link can now only ever be considered together with, never raced against, the default redirect. `use-push-registration.ts` keeps only the warm-tap listener (app already running). Regression tests in `boot-route.test.ts` (pure decision logic), the new `app/__tests__/index.test.tsx` (splash gating + integration), and `use-push-registration.test.tsx` (warm-tap listener, re-scoped from the removed cold-start tests). See docs/LC-D-REPORT-2026-08-03d.md. |

| LC-A05 | The 3 self-hosted Inter TTFs `src/ui/fonts.ts` loads (`Inter_400Regular`/`600SemiBold`/`700Bold`, ~342-344 KB each, ~1.03 MB total) ship each weight's full Google-Fonts charset (Latin+Cyrillic+Greek+Vietnamese extensions), but a repo-wide scan of every `.ts`/`.tsx` under `apps/mobile/src`+`app` found only 23 distinct non-ASCII codepoints ever literally rendered — all common Latin-1 punctuation/symbols (§ ° · × ÷ – — • … → ⇒ − ≈ ≤ ≥ ─ ★ ♂ ✓ ✕, plus a ZWJ/variation-selector pair and one emoji), zero non-Latin script. A real `pyftsubset` test subsetting to a generous Latin+symbols+emoji range (with headroom well beyond the 23 found codepoints) still cut each file 65.3% (342,408→118,960 / 343,632→119,168 / 344,072→119,260 bytes) with zero glyph loss against anything the app's own source currently renders. | `apps/mobile/src/ui/fonts.ts`, `@expo-google-fonts/inter` per-weight TTFs | LOW (bytes; needs a product call on dropping non-Latin-script glyph coverage for user-generated free text — names/notes/KYC fields — before landing) | **FIXED** (2026-08-03e) — `apps/mobile/scripts/subset-fonts.mjs` (pyftsubset, dev-time only) regenerates committed subset assets in `apps/mobile/assets/fonts/` from the ranges pinned in `scripts/font-safe-ranges.mjs` (Latin script + common symbols/punctuation/math/arrows/box-drawing/dingbats/emoji — no Cyrillic/Greek/Vietnamese-extensions/CJK); `fonts.ts` now `require()`s these instead of importing `@expo-google-fonts/inter` (moved to devDependency). Product call recorded explicitly in `docs/APP-SIZE.md`: a free-text character outside the safe range shows a tofu box, unchanged from today's behavior for any character outside Inter's original charset — neither Shona nor Ndebele (Zimbabwe's other official languages) needs a script beyond the retained Latin Extended-A/B range. `scripts/check-font-charset.mjs` (wired into `pnpm lint`) fails CI if a future string introduces an out-of-range character. | LC-C06 | `send.tsx`'s debounced (500ms trailing) draft persist wasn't flushed before `submit()` fired the create-order request — an edit made just before tapping "Send to riders" could still be sitting in the pending debounce timer when the request went out. If the app was then killed before the timer fired (well within the 15s request timeout), the on-disk draft (whose `idempotencyNonce` + content derives the recovery-time dedup key) reflected the PRE-edit content. A manual resubmit after relaunch would recompute a DIFFERENT idempotencyKey than the one actually sent (same nonce, different content hash), missing the server's dedup and opening a SECOND live auction for what the customer intended as one order. Found by the LC loop C 2026-08-02 C-T1 audit (customer order journey trace, condition (c): app killed/relaunched at a step boundary). | `apps/mobile/app/send.tsx:441` | MEDIUM (narrow timing window; double-auction risk, not money-loss) | **FIXED** (2026-08-02) — `submit()` now cancels the pending debounce timer and synchronously flushes the exact in-memory field values to SecureStore via `saveDraft()` BEFORE firing `createOrder()`, closing the window instead of narrowing it. Regression test in the new `app/__tests__/send.test.tsx` (confirmed it fails against the pre-fix code — the mocked `SecureStore.setItemAsync` was never called before the request fired). See docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-02c.md. |
| LC-C07 | Rider `confirmDelivery`'s durable "delivered" terminal marker (`saveRiderJobTerminal`) is written only after a response (success or 409-reconciled) is processed — an app kill strictly between sending the request and receiving any response drops the delivered-acknowledgement/rate-the-sender screen on relaunch. The order itself is correctly `delivered` server-side (CAS-guarded, no data loss) and shows correctly in Trip History; only the terminal UX flow is skipped. Found by the LC loop C 2026-08-02 C-T1 audit. | `apps/mobile/app/rider/job.tsx:305`, `apps/api/src/orders/order-lifecycle.service.ts:343` | LOW (UX-only, no data/money defect) | **FIXED** (2026-08-03f) — `deliverM` now writes the terminal marker in `onMutate`, BEFORE `confirmDelivery` fires, instead of only in `onSuccess`/the 409-reconciled branch of `onError`. Safe by construction: `reconcileRiderJobTerminal` only ever PROMOTES a marker once the order has actually left the active feed, so a marker written for a request that in fact failed just sits inert while the order stays active. The provisional marker is rolled back only on a DEFINITIVE non-delivery signal — a 401 (wrong code), a 403 (lockout), or a 409 reconciled (via a direct `getOrder` check) to "still not delivered" — and left in place on a genuinely ambiguous failure (network error/timeout/5xx, or the reconciliation check itself failing), where the existing `hasActiveOrder` gate already makes an inert leftover marker harmless. Regression tests in the new `app/rider/__tests__/job.test.tsx` (confirmed the "kill mid-request, relaunch" case fails against the pre-fix code — no marker meant a dead-end "No active job" screen instead of the acknowledgement/rate-the-sender terminal — and added a second case pinning the 401 rollback). See docs/LC-C-REPORT-2026-08-03f.md. |
| LC-C08 | `order/[id].tsx`'s `selectM` (accept-an-offer) mutation's `onError` shows the identical muted "that rider was just taken" notice for both a genuine race-loss 409 AND a lost-response case where the customer's own pick actually landed server-side — self-heals within one render via `onSettled`'s unconditional invalidate (so never a stuck/incorrect end state), but a slow reconnect could make the misleading flash user-perceptible. | LC-C12 | C-T4 merchant order-intake audit: the server's `TrackingGateway.isMerchantOnline` presence check (which `sweepExpiredAcceptWindows` consults before treating a past-deadline `awaiting_accept` order as a genuine no-answer) checks whether any socket has joined `merchantQueueRoom(merchantId)` — but no merchant-app code anywhere ever emitted `WS_EVENTS.merchantQueueSubscribe` to join that room (`use-queue-poll.ts`'s own comment: "Lane C5 (kitchen realtime socket) hasn't merged yet"). `isMerchantOnline()` therefore returned `false` for every merchant, always, so every past-deadline order had its `acceptDeadlineAt` pushed forward by the "merchant is dark, don't punish them" branch instead of ever reaching `commitStaleCancellation` — silently defeating N-03's documented "unanswered merchant accept auto-cancels, never a silent hang" guarantee (D-13) for connected AND disconnected merchants alike, not just genuinely offline ones. | LC-C14 | C-T5 reconnect-semantics audit: `apps/mobile/src/realtime/socket.ts`'s `createSocket` passes a captured `auth: { token }` OBJECT to `io()`, not a callback — every one of the four mobile realtime hooks (order/board/job/location) builds its socket through it. Socket.IO's own internal auto-reconnect (fired on a bare network drop, independent of React) replays that SAME captured object on every retry, so if a dead zone outlasts the 900s (`ACCESS_TTL_SECONDS`) access-token TTL, the socket keeps retrying with a now-expired token — repeated failed handshakes — until an UNRELATED REST call (the `connect_error` handler's own `refetchOrder`/`healBoard`/`refetchJob`, or a poll fallback) happens to 401, drives the existing single-flight refresh, and rotates `session.accessToken` in `AuthContext`; only then does the hook's `[…, token, …]` effect dependency see a new value, tear down the stale socket, and rebuild one with the fresh token. Self-heals (never a permanent stuck state — every hook's own `connect_error` handler already fires a REST call every retry) and needs an outage longer than 15 minutes to ever trigger, so it doesn't meet the DEFECT bar (no lost work / dead end / double-apply), but it's a real, evidenced gap: `apps/merchant/app/lib/queue-socket.ts`'s `createMerchantQueueSocket` already fixed the identical shape via an `auth` CALLBACK (`(cb) => cb({ token: loadMerchantSession()?.accessToken ?? "" })`), with its own comment stating the exact rationale ("a reconnect after the original access token has rotated still authenticates with whatever's current") — the mobile app never got the matching fix. Found by the LC loop C 2026-08-03 C-T5 audit (realtime-hook reconnect-semantics trace). | `apps/mobile/src/realtime/socket.ts:12-13` (`createSocket`), `apps/merchant/app/lib/queue-socket.ts:19-24` (already-fixed sibling pattern) | LOW (self-heals; needs a >15min outage to ever trigger) | OPEN → LC-C (C-O10) |

### LC-A wire-bytes audit 2026-08-03 (A-T4)

Field-by-field trace (not live capture) of the customer order journey and one rider steady-state
hour against the real service/response-builder code — full arithmetic in
`docs/LC-A-REPORT-2026-08-03.md`. All four findings are byte-diet optimizations on already-correct
functionality (no fixed-this-run defects); each is ledgered OPEN and appended to Lane A's
optimization checklist, ranked by measured [data] impact.

| ID | Description | Area | Sev | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LC-A06 | `MerchantOrderResponse` (39 fields) is serialized unconditionally by `food-order.service.ts`'s `toResponse()` on every poll regardless of order phase, unlike the parcel `OrderSnapshot`'s `getSnapshot()`, which deliberately nulls out phase-irrelevant fields. Debt-ledger/refund/cash-handshake fields (11 of the 39) ride every response even when nowhere near those states — ≈500B of guaranteed null padding per poll, compounding directly with the already-known A-O9 food dual-poll finding (every extra poll pays this padding too). | `packages/shared/src/contracts.ts:945-1007`, `apps/api/src/merchant/food-order.service.ts:781-842` (vs. the existing conditional pattern at `apps/api/src/orders/orders.service.ts:887-905`) | LOW (bytes, zero functional risk) | OPEN → LC-A (A-O14) |
| LC-A07 | Three independent Socket.IO connections (board, job, location) run concurrently during an active rider job — `createSocket()` opens a fresh `io(...)` per call with no sharing/singleton, and the board socket stays mounted even after the rider navigates to the job screen (the underlying `(tabs)` stack screen is never unmounted). Each connection pays its own transport handshake and ~25s engine.io keepalive, tripling background keepalive chatter and reconnect-driven self-heal REST traffic on any blip. | `apps/mobile/src/realtime/socket.ts:12-13`, `apps/mobile/src/realtime/use-rider-board.ts:56`, `apps/mobile/src/realtime/use-rider-job-socket.ts:48`, `apps/mobile/src/realtime/use-rider-location.ts:64` | LOW (bytes/battery, not correctness) | OPEN → LC-A (A-O17) |
| LC-A08 | `apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/home.tsx`'s Home tab runs its own 30s `refetchInterval` poll of `/orders/mine/active-order`, plus force-invalidates on every focus and foreground event, duplicating the same logical data `useBootstrap` already seeds from `/app/bootstrap` at cold start. A customer lingering on Home (the common launcher-screen case) before deciding to order pays 2+ extra redundant round trips/minute — the same redundant-polling shape A-O10 already covers for config, here for order-state. | `apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/home.tsx:121-137` | LOW (bytes, zero functional risk) | OPEN → LC-A (A-O15) |
| LC-A09 | Google Places autocomplete/details calls (`places.ts`, wired from `AddressSearch.tsx`'s 300ms debounce) are uncapped with no prefix-level response memoization — each settled keystroke pause during address entry fires a fresh ~1-2KB Google response with heavy content overlap against the immediately-prior call. Estimated ≈9.4 KB/order in address entry alone. Outside Lynia's own API/ETag layer (a direct Google REST call), so needs its own prefix-cache, not a server-side fix. | `apps/mobile/src/api/places.ts:62-75`, `apps/mobile/src/ui/AddressSearch.tsx:173` | LOW (bytes, third-party-bounded, zero functional risk) | OPEN → LC-A (A-O16) |

| Was | Now | PR |
|---|---|---|
| KB-PROD-DEPLOY-GATE — `release.yml`'s `build · migrate · deploy` job required a manual reviewer click on `environment: production` every run, wedging the deploy queue for hours at a time; recurred in every PR-health report since 2026-07-14 06:20 | **RESOLVED (observed 2026-07-17)** — the `3c9e55b6ae` run's deploy job sat `created` 01:57 UTC → `started` 05:30 UTC (~3.5h wait, consistent with the standing gate), but every release run since (`210eb6bd67` at 05:36, `ec7d008` at 07:58) started its deploy job within ~1 minute of being queued — no reviewer wait. Someone applied this report's own recommendation and removed the required-reviewer rule on the `production` GitHub Environment between those two runs. Not a code change, no PR. | n/a (Environment setting) |
| KB-CI-AUDIT-410 — `security` job hard-failed on every PR/push because npmjs.org retired both `pnpm audit` endpoints (410, `ERR_PNPM_AUDIT_BAD_RESPONSE`), unrelated to any real advisory; blocked auto-merge repo-wide since 2026-07-15 00:15, and four consecutive routine runs had a narrowly-scoped patch withheld by their own safety classifier since it touched a security gate unattended | **FIXED (human-approved)** — replaced `pnpm audit` entirely with `osv-scanner` (queries the OSV.dev vulnerability database directly, no dependency on the retired npm endpoint), fetched as a checked binary (verified against its published `SHA256SUMS`) pinned to `v2.4.0`, scanning `pnpm-lock.yaml` with `--min-severity=7` to reproduce the old `audit-level high` threshold (CVSS 7.0+ = High/Critical). Landed with explicit human sign-off in an interactive session after the classifier required it for this security-gate change. | #(this PR) |
| KB-CI-EXPR-EMPTY — `release.yml` (prod) and `deploy-staging.yml` silently no-op'd on **every** push to main from 2026-07-27 to 2026-07-29 (10 red runs, zero notifications). The 07-27 hardening commit `8617d95` added prose comments *inside* `run:` blocks containing a literal empty Actions expression token. Actions interpolates the whole script before bash sees it — a `#` shell comment is not a comment to the expression evaluator — so an empty expression failed the **workflow** to compile: runs completed in the same second with **zero jobs**, titled by file path because the `name:` was never parsed. Invisible to every guard: CI is a separate workflow so main looked green; `deploy-autoheal.yml` could not fire because `workflow_run.workflows` matches on the `name:` GitHub never parsed; the PR-health watchdog only looks at PRs. Note the API reported these as plain `failure`, **not** `startup_failure` — job count was the only reliable signal | **FIXED** — reworded the three offending comments (`deploy-staging.yml` ×1, `release.yml` ×2) to drop the literal token, with an inline warning against reintroducing it. **Prevention:** new `actionlint · workflow syntax` job in `ci.yml` compiles every expression the way Actions does, so the class is blocked at PR time (verified: exit 1 on the bug, 0 when clean; shellcheck/pyflakes deliberately disabled so pre-existing style nits don't become merge blockers). **Detection:** new hourly `workflow-startup-watchdog.yml` compiles main directly *and* classifies red runs by job count (not by `conclusion`, which was wrong here), escalating onto the existing `deploy-failure` label — it covers the quiet-period case where a broken workflow emits no runs at all. **Follow-up (same day):** the watchdog's first live run filed a false-positive (#419) — Check B escalated on the four *pre-fix* incident runs because they were still inside its 24h lookback, and would have re-filed hourly until the window rolled off. Fixed by judging each flagged workflow by its **newest** run on main (still zero-job-red = broken; recovered/green/in-flight = skip, deduped per workflow), so a historical failure whose fix has landed no longer pages. Both directions verified against stubbed API data | #(this PR) |
| KB-PROD-DEPLOY-GATE-RECUR — the `production` environment required-reviewer gate (see KB-PROD-DEPLOY-GATE, "resolved" 2026-07-17) is **back**: release run #298 sat `waiting` for a reviewer click from 2026-07-27 13:00 UTC until manually approved 2026-07-29 19:06 UTC (**54h**), and because `release.yml`/`rollback.yml` serialize on the `release-cloud-run` concurrency group it blocked every release behind it — ten merges to main shipped nothing. A `waiting` run is not red, so `deploy-autoheal.yml` never fires and the startup-watchdog's job-count checks don't see it; the only signal was the single review-request email. Each newer merge cancelled the previously queued release, so even the run list showed only `cancelled`, not a pile-up | **DETECTION ADDED (this PR); ROOT CAUSE is a repo setting, not code** — added Check C to `workflow-startup-watchdog.yml`: hourly scan for `status=waiting` runs on main older than `APPROVAL_STALL_THRESHOLD_HOURS` (default 4), which opens/refreshes a dedicated `deploy-failure` issue (own fixed title, independent open/close from the startup-failure issue) and auto-closes it once nothing is waiting. This makes the stall loud within an hour instead of silent for days. **To eliminate the class:** remove the required reviewer under Settings → Environments → production (staging gate + no-traffic canary + health-gated promotion + auto-rollback remain the safety net) — the same fix KB-PROD-DEPLOY-GATE applied before it regressed | #(this PR) |
| KB-F09 — counterparty phone revealed on terminal statuses forever | **FIXED** — `PHONE_REVEAL_STATUSES` drops `completed`; added `DISPUTE_PHONE_REVEAL_STATUSES` for ops | #196 |
| KB-F18b — notify-me at-most-once (waiter dropped on push failure) | **FIXED** — claim-lock + delivery-set clear only delivered → at-least-once, still de-duped | #197 |
| FRAUD P1-5 — issue-raise ops-DoS (no throttle) | **FIXED** — `@Throttle` on issue-raise | #195 |
| FRAUD P0-3 — penalty-free undelivered abandonment | **MITIGATED (velocity)** — auto-`on_hold` on abnormal undelivered rate (`UNDELIVERED_ABUSE`) | #198 |
| F-N3 — `/kyc/callback` unsigned in prod stub+manual | **FIXED** — fail-closed whenever prod or provider=didit | #199 |

| UX-0712 #6/#11 feed residue — in-app feed still showed generic "Order cancelled" for a rider-bail (live rebroadcast running) and "raise your price" for a no-supply expiry; #202 fixed push only | **FIXED** — feed rewrites the rider-bail row to the honest copy and routes its tap to the live clone (in-window derivation, zero extra queries); `expiry_no_supply` flag persisted at expiry (migration 0023) drives honest copy in feed + expired snapshot/terminal; feed also suppresses the canceller's own "cancelled" row, mirroring the push's actor exclusion | #206 |

## Interactive review 2026-07-26 — one-ID-one-account KYC hardening

User directive (2026-07-26): a person must NOT be able to hold accounts under different phone numbers
with one national ID — the previous A-04 posture (flag for the reviewer, never block; `schema.prisma`
"A FLAG, not a block") is superseded for LIVE accounts. This PR turns the duplicate-national-ID signal
into a hard gate at every point an ID is claimed, while keeping the flag as the reviewer signal for
what a block can't judge (erased-tombstone returning users + legacy pre-policy duplicates).
`pnpm typecheck` + full monorepo `test` green.

| ID | Description | Area | Sev | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IR26-01 | Duplicate national ID across live accounts was flag-only — a second SIM + same typed ID minted a working second account (KYC review was the only stop, and only when the vendor path was flagged). Now: claiming an ID already on another LIVE profile is refused with a 409 `id_in_use` at both ID-writing paths (`completeProfile`, `auth.updateProfile`) and at `becomeRider` (pre-vendor, so no paid Didit session is minted for a refused signup). Concurrent same-ID claimers are serialized with a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the ID hash inside each write tx (closes the count-then-write race). Erased tombstones (DS15-02b keeps `idNumberHash`) do NOT block — a restricted account can't self-erase (DS15-02), so a tombstone match is a legitimate returning user; it still sets `duplicateIdFlag`, `applyKycResult` still holds a flagged auto-verify (DOC-16-05), and `admin.getKycReview` still recomputes live. Resending your own stored ID is exempt (no new claim — legacy duplicates stay idempotent). | `apps/api/src/riders/rider.service.ts`, `apps/api/src/auth/auth.service.ts` | HIGH (user directive) | **FIXED (this PR)** — regression tests in `rider.service.spec.ts` + `auth.service.spec.ts` |
| IR26-02 | `becomeRider` accepted a profile with NO national ID: the dedup had nothing to key on, so an ID-less raw-API signup could reach vendor KYC entirely undeduped (there was even a spec asserting the collision query is skipped). Now a 400 — the national ID must be on the profile before rider onboarding (the stock client always writes it via `completeProfile` first, so only raw API callers ever see it). | `apps/api/src/riders/rider.service.ts` `becomeRider` | MEDIUM | **FIXED (this PR)** — test asserts 400 + no rider row |
| IR26-03 | `completeProfile` wrote `idNumber` without recomputing `rider.duplicateIdFlag` (only logged) — the exact DS-11 parity gap `auth.updateProfile` had fixed on its side; a post-onboarding ID rewrite through this route silently left a stale flag. Now recomputed and persisted on the rider row in the same tx (no-op for a not-yet-rider caller). | `apps/api/src/riders/rider.service.ts` `completeProfile` | LOW-MEDIUM | **FIXED (this PR)** — covered by the erased-tombstone + unique-ID tests |
| IR26-04 | **Vendor-document dedupe** (follow-up PR, user-directed): the IR26-01 gate keys off the number the applicant TYPED — a ban-evader's remaining move was typing a different/made-up number while showing the same real document to Didit. Now `extractDiditDocumentNumber` (defensive multi-path probe, same approach as `extractDiditScore`) pulls the document number out of the decision webhook; `applyKycResult` hashes it (`pii.hashId` normalizes, so it's directly comparable to `Profile.idNumberHash`), persists it as `Rider.verifiedIdHash` (migrations 0037/0038), and REFUSES to auto-verify when it mismatches the applicant's typed ID (or they have none — legacy) or collides with another account's typed or vendor-verified hash — held for manual review with a precise audit reason (`verified_id_mismatch` / `verified_id_collision`). `admin.getKycReview` matches collisions across BOTH hash axes and surfaces `verifiedIdMismatch` (tri-state) with a console warnbar. **Fail-open by design:** a payload without document data logs a coverage signal and degrades to pre-IR26-04 behavior, so a shape mismatch can never wedge real verifications — confirm extraction coverage on the first live Didit run (`KYC <ref>: verified webhook carried no document number`). `verifiedIdHash` is retained on erasure like `idNumberHash` (DS15-02b). | `apps/api/src/kyc/didit.ts`, `apps/api/src/riders/rider.service.ts` `applyKycResult`, `apps/api/src/admin/admin-kyc-review.service.ts`, `apps/admin/app/riders/[id]/kyc/page.tsx`, migrations 0037/0038 | MEDIUM-HIGH | **FIXED (this PR)** — tests in `didit.spec.ts`, `rider.service.spec.ts`, `kyc.controller.spec.ts`, `admin-kyc-review.service.spec.ts` |
| IR26-05 | **DB-level partial unique index on live `id_number_hash`** (follow-up PR, user-directed — was "deferred until prod data confirmed clean"): migration 0039 builds `profiles_live_id_number_hash_unique` CONCURRENTLY (repo online-safe convention, single-statement file) over `id_number_hash IS NOT NULL AND phone NOT LIKE 'erased:%'` — the exact predicate of the IR26-01 app-level block, so erased tombstones stay out of the unique scope. Both ID-write paths map its P2002 to the same 409 `id_in_use`. **Ops note (in the migration file):** if pre-#398 legacy live duplicates exist in prod, the CONCURRENT build fails and `migrate deploy` halts BEFORE the traffic move (running revision unaffected), leaving an INVALID index — resolve the duplicate accounts, `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS`, re-deploy; detection query included in the migration comment. | `apps/api/prisma/migrations/0039_live_national_id_unique/`, `rider.service.ts` `completeProfile`, `auth.service.ts` `updateProfile` | MEDIUM | **SHIPPED (this PR)** — migration-safety guard green; P2002 mapping covered by typecheck + existing 409 tests |

**Residual after IR26-04/05:** a ban-evader must now defeat the VENDOR (a genuinely different physical
document, or a Didit workflow that doesn't return document data — watch the coverage log line above).
The remaining identity work is unchanged: `KB-IDENTITY-BINDING` L3 hardware attestation + L0 destructive
rebind (both founder/vendor-gated).

## FIXED — latest sweep (BUGHUNT_FINDINGS.md F-01…F-19)

Fixed by PR #192 (`3f15c42`, F-01…F-10) and PR #193 (`f9c2a12`, F-11…F-19). Verified in code.

| ID | Description | Fix (verified) |
|---|---|---|
| F-01 | Banned/suspended rider retained sender-side (customer) access | `OrdersService.create` now reads profile standing and blocks on_hold/banned senders |
| F-02 | Stale GPS shown as "live" via `@updatedAt` fallback | Added `riders.position_updated_at` (migration 0022), used for snapshot freshness |
| F-03 | Delivery-OTP lockout resettable via unthrottled rotate | `@Throttle` on `delivery-code/rotate` route |
| F-04 | Shared-device cross-account leak (JOB_KEY, RIDER_BID_DRAFT_KEY) | `session.ts` clears both keys on sign-out |
| F-05 | Recipient dedupe fails across local vs +263 formats | Dedupe via shared E.164 `normalizePhone` |
| F-06 | Spoofable admin audit actor via `x-lynia-operator` | Middleware strips inbound header before re-asserting |
| F-07 | Tamperable admin follow-up-note hidden fields | Dedicated server action, hardcoded action + server-derived target |
| F-08 | On-hold rider recovery copy impossible to follow | Copy corrected to direct to support |
| F-10 | rankOffers NaN; normalizePhone implausible ZW lengths | NaN guard + ZW national-length tightening |
| F-11 | **Stored XSS** in ops console (dispute desc via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`) | Rendered through React escaping; no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` remains in `apps/admin` |
| F-12 | Uncaught reconciler crash (findMany outside try/catch, no process backstop) | Reconciler bodies try/caught; fire-and-forget carries `.catch`; `main.ts` adds `unhandledRejection`+`uncaughtException` handlers |
| F-13 | KYC resubmit cap unreachable in Didit auto mode | Auto/Didit decline increments `kycAttempts` inside monotonic-apply guard (replay-safe) |
| F-14 | KYC manual-decline non-idempotent | Manual decline guarded by `isRepeatOfSameDecline` against double-count |
| F-15 | OTP verify + 60s grace window unthrottled | `@Throttle` on `/auth/otp/verify`; grace path gains per-phone attempt ceiling |
| F-16 | Rider-role sender never gets presence:stale watchdog | Watchdog keys off per-order relationship, not global JWT role |
| F-17 | queue.add awaited on hot path; replay skipped broadcast | Enqueues `void ...catch` (not awaited); broadcast not gated behind enqueue |
| F-19 | `/client-metrics` had no rate limit | `@Throttle({limit:12,windowSec:60})` |

## FIXED / MOOT — older reports (deduped clusters)

**Money-fraud cluster → MOOT.** `settlements.service.ts` was rewritten to a read-only prepaid
per-ride model; `recordPayment`, `adjustFare`-driven settlement, refund-netting and auto-pause
mechanics no longer exist, so these no longer apply: FRAUD P0-2 commission-no-floor,
P0-3 undelivered-excluded-from-base, P1-1 refund-nets-whole-week, P2-1 admin-adjustfare-downward,
P2-2 recordpayment-no-proof; BUG-HUNT p2-4 recordpayment-no-audit, p3 adjustfare-no-status-guard;
BUGFIX P2-4, LAUNCH-FIX recordPayment-CAS. (Prepaid wallet itself is a future build.)

**Object-authz / IDOR cluster → FIXED (verified).** self-dealing wash-trade (makeOffer rejects
`customerId===riderId`), banned-rider-bidding (onlineRefusalReason standing gate), offers
`listForOrder` ownership gate, order `getSnapshot` party gate, offer TOCTOU (FOR UPDATE tx),
logout profileId-scoping, riders/nearby role-gate, boardLeave geo-room leave. (FRAUD P0-1/P2-3,
BUG-HUNT p2-1/p2-2/p3-offers-idor/p3-logout-idor/p3-nearby-enum/p2-7, ENG B-P0-3.)

**Auth/identity cluster → FIXED (verified).** JWT default-secret prod boot-guard, JWT HS256
algorithm pin, `x-user-id` dev fallback allowlist, OTP verify TOCTOU / concurrent-refresh atomic
rotation, launch-mode boot guards (console OTP, test phones, stub KYC rejected in prod).
(BUG-HUNT p1-1/p3-xuserid/p3-jwt-alg, ENG B-P0-1/B-P0-2, SECURITY P0-1/P2-3/P2-4, FRAUD P1-4.)

**KYC cluster → FIXED.** unsigned-webhook-fail-open (503 when provider=didit & no secret),
`applyKycResult` updateMany→unique `kyc_ref` (migration 0005), replay/reorder monotonic
`kyc_resolved_at` guard, vendor-outage 503 mapping, silent-stub loud warnings, KYC retry brute
force (now capped via F-13). (ENG KYC-P0-1/P0-2/P1-1/P1-2/P2-1, FRAUD P1-2, BUG-HUNT p3-kyc-callback:
stub-mode-only, rejected in prod by launch guard.)

**Notifications/FCM cluster → FIXED.** dead-token pruning (PushResult invalidToken + deleteMany),
device-token full-logging masked, batched sendEach ≤500, rider-broadcast push on open_for_offers,
device-token rehoming guard. (ENG FCM-P1/P2-1..4, BUG-HUNT p3-device-token-rehoming.)

**Data-integrity cluster → FIXED.** reports unique NULL order_id (migration 0014), rankOffers NaN
guard, DB check constraints (migration 0015), national-ID encryption at rest AES-GCM
(migration 0017). Two-way rating unique discriminator resolved. (BUG-HUNT p3 batch, F-10.)

**Edge/abuse cluster → FIXED.** global ThrottleGuard + @Throttle, Helmet security headers, CORS
allow-list, Socket.IO CORS tightened, global exception filter, 1MB body cap, outbound fetch
timeouts (Didit/WhatsApp/admin/apiFetch). (SECURITY P1-2/P1-3, ENG SHIP/B-P1, UX 07-08 #6.)

**Mobile journey dead-ends → FIXED.** markUndelivered flow, cancel-gate, cold-start restore
(customer + rider + 24h handback), push deep-link routing, resend-OTP cooldown, rebroadcast
prefill, MapPicker denied/timeout, error-state honesty, statuspill labels, on_hold copy,
StatusPill tones, PickupChecklist dead-end, stale cold-start push replay, bid-draft persistence.
(JOURNEY-BUGS R1–R10/C1–C12/S1, UX passes 07-08…07-11, TAIL-HARDENING R8.)

**Ship/infra correctness → FIXED.** WS LB timeout 3600, Cloud Run request timeout 3600, VPC
connector to Memorystore, dedicated run SA, keyless V4 signBlob, WIF keyless deploy, release.yml
config validation. (ENG SHIP-3a/3b/3c.)

## Coverage map — where past sweeps concentrated

**Heavily audited (multiple sweeps, deep):**
`auth`/OTP, `orders`/`order-lifecycle`, `offers`/`matching`, `settlements`, `kyc`, `riders`
(reliability/gates), `tracking.gateway` (rooms/presence/GPS), `admin` (audit/authz/cancel/fare),
`issues` (XSS/disputes), `notifications`/FCM, `reports`/blocks, `config`/env boot guards, mobile
customer + rider journeys, infra/terraform provisioning.

**Lightly or never audited before this sweep (Phase-1 hunting ground):**
`sos`, `privacy` (erasure/retention), `uploads` + signed-URL scope, `health`, `observability`
(metrics interceptor / client-metrics / otel), adapters (`gcs.storage`, `fcm.push`, `secrets`),
`common` (throttle guard ordering, exception filter, zod pipe, trust-proxy), `phone-backfill`,
admin-audit internals, `tracking.service` geo/Redis internals beyond the gateway.

**Now covered (2026-07-15, wallet & data-lifecycle audit — first run of the `WD-` lane):** the prepaid
commission wallet end to end — `wallet.controller`/`wallet.service` (top-up lifecycle, the credit
primitive, the per-ride debit), the admin financial surfaces (`admin-orders.service.adjustFare`,
`wallet.service.creditManual`, `settlements.service.commissionOverview`), and the rider-facing earnings
tab. See the dedicated section below for findings WD-001…WD-011.

**Extended (2026-07-16, wallet & data-lifecycle audit — second WD run):** the commission-basis floor
(fare-lowballing evasion), the `CommissionLedger` unique-index/`orderId` invariant (now a partial index —
`adjustment` rows carry real `orderId`s), the KYC auto-verify webhook's interaction with the A-04
duplicate-ID flag, `admin-customers.service` (`cancelRatePct` attribution), and the rider-detail /
cash-page wallet copy & figures. See the dedicated section below for findings WD-012…WD-017 plus the
